# Skills · P · part 155

847 entries, psk-blueprint-advisor to ptg-release-deployment-guard. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **psk-blueprint-advisor**: Place a team on the Productside Blueprint, coach a phase, or audit a decision against the five Optimal Product Process Principles. Use when unsure which phase you are in or what to run next. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-blueprint-advisor))
- **psk-empathy-interviews**: Prepare empathy interview questions, get conduct guidance, and document structured findings using the Productside empathy interview framework. Use when you need to validate assumptions about customer needs through direct conversation. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-empathy-interviews))
- **psk-exploring-solution-space**: Prepare a design thinking session to brainstorm and evaluate solution hypotheses using the Productside method catalog. Use when you have a validated problem statement and need to explore the solution space before committing to a direction, or when asked to generate solution ideas for a problem. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-exploring-solution-space))
- **psk-framing-the-problem**: Frame a validated customer problem using the Productside stem sequence (I am / Trying to / But / Because / Which makes me feel) and craft a clear problem statement. Use when you have enough discovery evidence to define what problem is worth solving. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-framing-the-problem))
- **psk-go-to-market**: Build a Go-to-Market Enablement artifact that aligns sales, marketing, support, and technical teams before launch. Use when the PM needs to communicate what is launching, who it is for, why it matters, and what each team should say about it, or when asked to write launch messages, add testimonials, or set launch goals. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-go-to-market))
- **psk-jobs-to-be-done**: Identify customer Jobs-to-Be-Done and desired Product Outcomes using Productside job statement formats. Two steps - Step 1 enumerates job statements and personas (pre-discovery), Step 2 fills in product outcomes (often after empathy interviews). Use when you need to articulate what customers are trying to accomplish before jumping to solutions. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-jobs-to-be-done))
- **psk-lofi-experimentation**: Design a lo-fidelity experiment to test a critical solution assumption in Productside format. Use when you have a solution hypothesis and need to validate it with minimal time and effort before building. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-lofi-experimentation))
- **psk-market-selection-sizing**: Name a market, run a PESTEL on it, cut it into at least two segments with the users, buyers, and influencers in each, and size TAM, SAM, and SOM with a source behind every input. Use before jobs and persona work, when the team has not yet named who it serves or whether the opportunity is worth exploring. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-market-selection-sizing))
- **psk-measuring-outcomes**: Build a post-launch outcomes dashboard that ties product and business outcomes to specific metrics, measurement tools, and baseline data. Use after launch to track whether the product is delivering on its promises. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-measuring-outcomes))
- **psk-outcome-roadmap**: Build an outcome-based roadmap and release plan that connects product outcomes to outputs, sorted by confidence level. Use when a PM needs to communicate what the team is pursuing and why, in language that works for both executives and engineering. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-outcome-roadmap))
- **psk-outcome-strategy-discussion**: Facilitate a structured PM-manager alignment conversation to confirm the business outcome, context, and success criteria before discovery begins. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-outcome-strategy-discussion))
- **psk-outcome-tree**: Connect business outcomes to product outcomes using the Productside Outcome Tree. Two modalities - Section One (pre-discovery business context) and Section Two (post-discovery actuals vs. planned). Use when you need to establish or update the strategic link between what the business is trying to achieve and what the product must deliver. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-outcome-tree))
- **psk-output-prioritization**: Prepare to facilitate, then record, a team ranking of product outputs (features, capabilities) by relative customer value and business cost on a four-quadrant grid anchored on a center-point output. Use when a team needs to sequence what to build first and communicate tradeoffs transparently before committing to a roadmap, or when asked to place features in quadrants or name a top N. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-output-prioritization))
- **psk-positioning-statement**: Craft a Productside-style positioning statement using the For/Who/Is a/That/Unlike/Our product gives formula, informed by competitive analysis. Use when you need to articulate your product's unique value proposition and differentiation. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-positioning-statement))
- **psk-problem-identification**: Identify customer jobs, pains, gains, and candidate problem statements in Productside format, or review drafted problem statements ("X gets in the way of achieving Y"). Use when you need compact, evidence-backed problem candidates from customer research, or want drafted statements checked. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-problem-identification))
- **psk-process-retrospective**: Help a PM prepare for, facilitate documentation of, and close out a team retrospective using the Productside nautical four-quadrant model. Use when a team needs to reflect on a completed phase, sprint, launch, or project and produce concrete action items. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-process-retrospective))
- **psk-product-outcome-canvas**: Consolidate all discovery and define artifacts into the Product Outcome Canvas, the Go/No Go decision document presented to leadership before entering the Create phase. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-product-outcome-canvas))
- **psk-product-readiness**: Design and document a structured Product Readiness Test plan before launch. Use when a PM needs to plan a beta program, define Go/No Go criteria, and capture assumptions and risks that are out of scope for the test. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-product-readiness))
- **psk-proto-persona**: Create a Productside-style proto-persona for a primary user, then optionally a buyer persona, or review a finished research-based persona before sharing it. Use when you need a working customer profile before deeper validation, or want a persona sharpened for stakeholders, polished for a readout, or given more quotes. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-proto-persona))
- **psk-risk-assessment**: Identify, prioritize, and plan for product risks in Productside format. Use when you need to surface potential threats to product success, classify them by severity and likelihood, and build mitigation and contingency plans before committing to a build or launch. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-risk-assessment))
- **psk-solution-hypothesis**: Craft a testable solution hypothesis using the If/Then structure, producing both a customer-focused and a business-focused hypothesis that chain together, grounded in a validated problem statement. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-solution-hypothesis))
- **psk-stakeholder-alignment**: Map stakeholders by role, interest, and influence, then assign engagement strategies before discovery begins. Use also when asked to place named people on a power/interest grid. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-stakeholder-alignment))
- **psk-storyboarding**: Build a six-panel narrative storyboard that validates a product solution hypothesis by telling a customer-centered story from problem to lasting change. Use during the Create phase, after the solution hypothesis is in hand. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-storyboarding))
- **psk-tiny-acts-discovery**: Run continuous assumption validation through small, fast tests. Use at any phase of the product lifecycle to keep the team calibrated on what they know, what they don't, and what to test next. ([deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills](https://github.com/deanpeters/ps-playbook-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/psk-tiny-acts-discovery))
- **pskill**: Resume, inspect, pause, or cancel a pskill run (a programmatic skill run). Use when the user mentions an unfinished skill run, or asks which runs exist. ([guplem/mystery-forge](https://github.com/guplem/mystery-forge/tree/HEAD/generator/.agents/skills/pskill) · ★1 · 4 copies)

- **pslog-planning**: pslog로 관리되는 프로젝트에서 새 feature 아이디어를 기술적으로 탄탄한 기획으로 만드는 워크플로. 사용자가 "새 기능/이거 만들자/기획하자" 류로 들어올 때 사용. 5렌즈(문제·차별/범위/설계/adversarial/테스트)로 실행계획.md를 쓰고 PLAN.md task로 분해한다. task 하나를 코드로 옮기는 건 pslog-workflow 스킬. - **pslog-refactor**: pslog로 관리되는 프로젝트에서 행동은 그대로 두고 구조만 손보는 동작 보존 리팩토링 워크플로. "이거 정리하자/리팩토링/중복 제거/구조 개선/이 파일 지저분한데" 류로 들어올 때 사용. 코드 진단→무게(light/heavy)→범위 확정→동작보존 계약까지 정리한 뒤 pslog-workflow 로 코드화한다. (행동을 *바꾸는* 버그픽스/기능변경은 pslog-workflow, 새 기능 기획은 pslog-planning.) ([ardenspace/pslog](https://github.com/ardenspace/pslog/tree/HEAD/plugins/pslog-workflow/skills/pslog-refactor))
- **pslog-workflow**: pslog로 관리되는 프로젝트에서 할당된 task를 탄탄하게 실현하는 워크플로. 사용자가 "내 할 일/다음 작업/뭐 하지" 류로 묻거나, task를 잡고 코드 작성에 들어갈 때, 또는 행동을 *바꾸는* 버그픽스/기능변경에 사용.
